---db:T18f6,### TEST-01: Operator Precedence & "Of" Grouping
* **Target Domain**: Simplification & Arithmetic Expressions
* **V1 Error (ERR-01)**: Claiming division precedes multiplication and labeling $20 \div 5 \times 2 = 8$ as a "fatal exam error".
* **Correct Mathematical Invariant**:
  $$\text{Multiplication and Division share equal precedence, evaluated strictly left to right.}$$
  $$20 \div 5 \times 2 = (20 \div 5) \times 2 = 4 \times 2 = 8$$
  $$\text{"Of" is an exam grouping convention:} \quad 20 \div 5 \text{ of } 2 = 20 \div (5 \times 2) = 20 \div 10 = 2$$
* **Verification Code**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual((20 / 5) * 2, 8);
  assert.strictEqual(20 / (5 * 2), 2);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST-02: Modulo-100 Power Cyclicity ($76 \times 2^k$)
* **Target Domain**: Modular Arithmetic & Last Two Digits
* **V1 Error (ERR-02)**: Stating unconditionally that $76 \times 2^k \equiv 2^k \pmod{100}$.
* **Counterexample**:
  $$k = 1: \quad 76 \times 2^1 = 152 \equiv 52 \pmod{100} \quad \text{while } 2^1 = 02 \pmod{100} \quad (52 \neq 02)$$
* **Rigorous Domain Boundary**:
  $$\text{Valid strictly for integer } k \ge 2:$$
  $$(76 - 1) \times 2^k = 75 \times 2^k = 75 \times 4 \times 2^{k-2} = 300 \times 2^{k-2} \equiv 0 \pmod{100}$$
  $$\implies 76 \times 2^k \equiv 2^k \pmod{100} \quad \forall k \ge 2$$
* **Verification Results**:
  * $k=1: 76 \times 2 = 152 \equiv 52 \neq 2$ **(FAILS - Counterexample Proved)**
  * $k=2: 76 \times 4 = 304 \equiv 04 \equiv 2^2$ **(PASSES)**
  * $k=3: 76 \times 8 = 608 \equiv 08 \equiv 2^3$ **(PASSES)**
  * $k=4: 76 \times 16 = 1216 \equiv 16 \equiv 2^4$ **(PASSES)**
  * $k=5: 76 \times 32 = 2432 \equiv 32 \equiv 2^5$ **(PASSES)**
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST-03: Quadratic Root Comparison ($a_1 \neq a_2$)
* **Target Domain**: Quadratic Equations & Inequalities
* **V1 Error (ERR-16)**: Shortcut claiming that when $a_1 \neq a_2$, one can multiply already-divided roots by opposing coefficients ($X = x \times a_2, Y = y \times a_1$) to compare.
* **Counterexample**:
  $$\text{Eq 1: } x^2 - 9x + 20 = 0 \implies x_1 = 4, x_2 = 5 \quad (a_1 = 1)$$
  $$\text{Eq 2: } 10y^2 - 130y + 420 = 0 \implies y_1 = 6, y_2 = 7 \quad (a_2 = 10)$$
  $$\text{True Relationship: } x_i \in \{4, 5\} < y_j \in \{6, 7\} \implies \mathbf{x < y}$$
  $$\text{Applying Flawed Cross-Scaling to Roots:}$$
  $$X \in \{4 \times 10, 5 \times 10\} = \{40, 50\}, \quad Y \in \{6 \times 1, 7 \times 1\} = \{6, 7\}$$
  $$\implies \mathbf{X > Y} \quad \text{[CATASTROPHIC ERROR! Inverts truth from } x < y \text{ to } x > y\text{]}$$
* **Correct Mathematical Method**:
  1. Direct Decimal/Fractional Comparison: Compare $x_i$ and $y_j$ directly on the real number line.
  2. Common Denominator on Factor Numerators: For roots $\alpha = N_x/a_1$ and $\beta = N_y/a_2$, compare $N_x \times a_2$ with $N_y \times a_1$ *before* division.
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST-04: Quadratic Instant CND Invariance
* **Target Domain**: Root Sign Analysis
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  $$\text{If } a_1 > 0, c_1 < 0 \text{ and } a_2 > 0, c_2 < 0 \implies \text{Discriminants } D_1, D_2 > 0 \text{ and roots straddle zero.}$$
* **Formal Proof**:
  * Product of roots $\alpha_1 \beta_1 = c_1/a_1 < 0 \implies x_1 > 0 > x_2$.
  * Product of roots $\alpha_2 \beta_2 = c_2/a_2 < 0 \implies y_1 > 0 > y_2$.
  * We have $x_1 > y_2$ (positive $>$ negative) and $y_1 > x_2$ (positive $>$ negative).
  * Neither $x \ge y$ nor $y \ge x$ can hold globally across all root pairs.
  * Therefore, the relationship is **Cannot Be Determined (CND)** with $100\%$ mathematical certainty.
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST-05: Rhombus Geometry & Pythagorean Verification
* **Target Domain**: 2D Mensuration
* **V1 Error (ERR-17)**: Scratch text leaked into manuscript causing conflicting side/area values.
* **Verified Parameters**:
  * Diagonal $d_1 = 32\text{ cm}$
  * Area $= \frac{1}{2} d_1 d_2 = 2016\text{ cm}^2$
  * $d_2 = \frac{2 \times 2016}{32} = 126\text{ cm}$
  * Half-diagonals: $p = 16\text{ cm}$, $q = 63\text{ cm}$
  * Side $a = \sqrt{16^2 + 63^2} = \sqrt{256 + 3969} = \sqrt{4225} = 65\text{ cm}$
  * Perimeter $P = 4a = 4 \times 65 = 260\text{ cm}$
* **Verification Code**:
  ```javascript
  const d1 = 32, area = 2016;
  const d2 = (2 * area) / d1; // 126
  const a = Math.sqrt(Math.pow(d1 / 2, 2) + Math.pow(d2 / 2, 2)); // 65
  const perimeter = 4 * a; // 260
  assert.strictEqual(d2, 126);
  assert.strictEqual(a, 65);
  assert.strictEqual(perimeter, 260);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST-06: Dishonest Merchant Universal Multiplier
* **Target Domain**: Commercial Arithmetic
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  $$M_{\text{net}} = M_{\text{markup}} \times M_{\text{discount}} \times M_{\text{weight}}$$
  $$\text{Example: Markup } 20\%, \text{ Discount } 10\%, \text{ Uses } 800\text{g for } 1000\text{g:}$$
  $$M_{\text{net}} = \frac{120}{100} \times \frac{90}{100} \times \frac{1000}{800} = 1.2 \times 0.9 \times 1.25 = 1.35 \implies \mathbf{35\% \text{ Net Profit}}$$
* **Verification Code**:
  ```javascript
  const m = 1.2 * 0.9 * (1000 / 800);
  assert.strictEqual(m, 1.35);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST-07: Semicircle & Hemisphere Closed Boundary Invariants
* **Target Domain**: Spatial Mensuration
* **Semicircle Perimeter**:
  $$P = \pi r + 2r = r\left(\frac{22}{7} + 2\right) = \frac{36}{7}r$$
  $$\text{For } r = 14\text{ cm}: \quad P = \frac{36}{7} \times 14 = 72\text{ cm} \quad (\text{not } 44\text{ cm})$$
* **Solid Hemisphere TSA**:
  $$\text{TSA} = 2\pi r^2 + \pi r^2 = 3\pi r^2$$
  $$\text{For } r = 7\text{ cm}: \quad \text{TSA} = 3 \times \frac{22}{7} \times 49 = 462\text{ cm}^2 \quad (\text{not } 308\text{ cm}^2)$$
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST-08: Kinematic Meeting Times Invariant
* **Target Domain**: Time, Speed & Distance
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  $$\frac{v_A}{v_B} = \sqrt{\frac{T_B}{T_A}}, \quad t_{\text{meeting}} = \sqrt{T_A T_B}$$
  $$\text{For } T_A = 4\text{ hours}, T_B = 9\text{ hours}:$$
  $$\frac{v_A}{v_B} = \sqrt{\frac{9}{4}} = \frac{3}{2} = 1.5, \quad t_{\text{meeting}} = \sqrt{4 \times 9} = 6\text{ hours}$$
* **Verification Code**:
  ```javascript
  const TA = 4, TB = 9;
  const ratio = Math.sqrt(TB / TA);
  const t_meet = Math.sqrt(TA * TB);
  assert.strictEqual(ratio, 1.5);
  assert.strictEqual(t_meet, 6);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

```dd:T2422,### TEST 01: Operator Precedence & "Of" Grouping
* **Target Domain**: Simplification & Arithmetic Expressions (Ch 02)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-01
* **Correct Mathematical Invariant**:
  Multiplication and Division share equal precedence, evaluated strictly left to right.
  $$20 \div 5 \times 2 = (20 \div 5) \times 2 = 4 \times 2 = 8$$
  "Of" represents an Indian competitive exam grouping convention with highest precedence:
  $$20 \div 5 \text{ of } 2 = 20 \div (5 \times 2) = 20 \div 10 = 2$$
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual((20 / 5) * 2, 8);
  assert.strictEqual(20 / (5 * 2), 2);
  assert.strictEqual(((180 / 15) / 3) * 4, 16);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST 02: Modulo-100 Power Cyclicity ($76 \times 2^k$)
* **Target Domain**: Modular Arithmetic & Last Two Digits (Ch 07)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-02
* **Counterexample**:
  For $k = 1$: $76 \times 2^1 = 152 \equiv 52 \pmod{100}$, whereas $2^1 = 02 \pmod{100}$ ($52 \neq 02$).
* **Domain Boundary**:
  Valid strictly for integer $k \ge 2$:
  $$(76 - 1) \times 2^k = 75 \times 2^k = 75 \times 4 \times 2^{k-2} = 300 \times 2^{k-2} \equiv 0 \pmod{100}$$
  $$\implies 76 \times 2^k \equiv 2^k \pmod{100} \quad \forall k \ge 2$$
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 03: Quadratic Root Comparison Counterexample ($a_1 \neq a_2$)
* **Target Domain**: Quadratic Equations & Root Comparison (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-16
* **Counterexample to Root Cross-Scaling**:
  Consider $x^2 - 9x + 20 = 0 \implies x \in \{4, 5\}$ ($a_1 = 1$).
  And $10y^2 - 130y + 420 = 0 \implies y \in \{6, 7\}$ ($a_2 = 10$).
  True relation: $x < y$ (both $4, 5 < 6, 7$).
  Flawed shortcut: multiply already-divided roots by opposing leading coefficients:
  $X = \{4 \times 10, 5 \times 10\} = \{40, 50\}$, $Y = \{6 \times 1, 7 \times 1\} = \{6, 7\}$.
  This inverts the true relation to $X > Y$, causing a catastrophic exam blunder.
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST 04: Quadratic Negative Constant CND Invariant
* **Target Domain**: Quadratic Inequalities (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-04
* **Formal Proof of Root Straddling**:
  For $a_1 x^2 + b_1 x - c_1 = 0$ and $a_2 y^2 + b_2 y - c_2 = 0$ with $a_1, a_2 > 0$ and $c_1, c_2 > 0$:
  Product of roots $P_x = -c_1/a_1 < 0 \implies$ one positive root $x_1 > 0$, one negative root $x_2 < 0$.
  Product of roots $P_y = -c_2/a_2 < 0 \implies$ one positive root $y_1 > 0$, one negative root $y_2 < 0$.
  Comparing positive $x_1$ with negative $y_2$: $x_1 > 0 > y_2 \implies x_1 > y_2$.
  Comparing positive $y_1$ with negative $x_2$: $y_1 > 0 > x_2 \implies y_1 > x_2 \implies x_2 < y_1$.
  Since $x_1 > y_2$ requires $x > y$ while $x_2 < y_1$ requires $x < y$, neither $x \ge y$ nor $x \le y$ can hold.
  Hence the relationship **Cannot Be Determined (CND)**.
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST 05: Rhombus Pythagorean Integrity
* **Target Domain**: 2D Mensuration & Geometry (Ch 22)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-17
* **Calculation**:
  $d_1 = 32\text{ cm}$, $\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} d_1 d_2 = 2016 \implies d_2 = \frac{4032}{32} = 126\text{ cm}$.
  Half-diagonals: $\frac{d_1}{2} = 16\text{ cm}$, $\frac{d_2}{2} = 63\text{ cm}$.
  Side $a = \sqrt{16^2 + 63^2} = \sqrt{256 + 3969} = \sqrt{4225} = 65\text{ cm}$.
  Perimeter $= 4a = 4 \times 65 = 260\text{ cm}$.
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 06: Dishonest Dealer Universal Multiplier
* **Target Domain**: Commercial Arithmetic (Ch 14)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-08
* **Universal Multiplier**:
  $$\text{Multiplier} = M_{\text{markup}} \times M_{\text{discount}} \times M_{\text{weight}} = \left(\frac{100 + m}{100}\right) \times \left(\frac{100 - d}{100}\right) \times \left(\frac{\text{Claimed Weight}}{\text{Actual Dispensed Weight}}\right)$$
  For $m = 20\%, d = 10\%, \text{weight} = 800\text{g for } 1000\text{g}$:
  $$\text{Multiplier} = 1.20 \times 0.90 \times \frac{1000}{800} = 1.08 \times 1.25 = 1.35 \implies 35\% \text{ Profit}$$
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 11: Quadratic Sign Table Root Invariant (Algebraic Value vs Absolute Magnitude)
* **Target Domain**: Quadratic Equations (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-19
* **Mathematical Proof**:
  For $x^2 + bx - c = 0$ ($b, c > 0$), roots are $\frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2 + 4c}}{2}$.
  Since $\sqrt{b^2 + 4c} > b$, the roots are $+x_{\text{pos}}$ and $-x_{\text{neg}}$.
  On the real number line, $x_{\text{pos}} > 0 > -x_{\text{neg}}$, so the positive root is algebraically greater.
  In terms of absolute magnitude, $|-x_{\text{neg}}| = \frac{b + \sqrt{b^2+4c}}{2} > \frac{-b + \sqrt{b^2+4c}}{2} = x_{\text{pos}}$.
  Test Case: $x^2 + 2x - 3 = 0 \implies$ roots $+1, -3$.
  Algebraic: $+1 > -3$. Magnitude: $|-3| = 3 > 1$.
* **Status**: **PASSED**

  Counterexample to unqualified sign rule: $x^2 + x + 1 = 0$ has $b = 1 > 0, c = 1 > 0$, but $D = 1 - 4 = -3 < 0$, so no real roots exist.

```df:T2908,### TEST 01: Operator Precedence & "Of" Grouping
* **Target Domain**: Simplification & Arithmetic Expressions (Ch 02)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-01
* **Correct Mathematical Invariant**:
  Multiplication and Division share equal precedence, evaluated strictly left to right:
  $$20 \div 5 \times 2 = (20 \div 5) \times 2 = 4 \times 2 = 8$$
  "Of" represents an Indian competitive exam grouping convention with highest precedence:
  $$20 \div 5 \text{ of } 2 = 20 \div (5 \times 2) = 20 \div 10 = 2$$
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual((20 / 5) * 2, 8);
  assert.strictEqual(20 / (5 * 2), 2);
  assert.strictEqual(((180 / 15) / 3) * 4, 16);
  ```

### TEST 02: Modulo-100 Power Cyclicity ($76 \times 2^k$)
* **Target Domain**: Modular Arithmetic & Last Two Digits (Ch 07)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-02
* **Counterexample**:
  For $k = 1$: $76 \times 2^1 = 152 \equiv 52 \pmod{100}$, whereas $2^1 = 02 \pmod{100}$ ($52 \neq 02$).
* **Domain Boundary**:
  Valid strictly for integer $k \ge 2$:
  $$(76 - 1) \times 2^k = 75 \times 2^k = 75 \times 4 \times 2^{k-2} = 300 \times 2^{k-2} \equiv 0 \pmod{100}$$
  $$\implies 76 \times 2^k \equiv 2^k \pmod{100} \quad \forall k \ge 2$$
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual((76 * 2) % 100, 52);
  assert.notStrictEqual((76 * 2) % 100, 2);
  for (let k = 2; k <= 10; k++) {
    const val = (BigInt(76) * (BigInt(2) ** BigInt(k))) % 100n;
    const direct = (BigInt(2) ** BigInt(k)) % 100n;
    assert.strictEqual(val, direct);
  }
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 03: Quadratic Root Comparison Counterexample ($a_1 \neq a_2$)

  And $10y^2 - 130y + 420 = 0 \implies y \in \{6, 7\}$ ($a_2 = 10$).
  True relation is $x < y$ for all root pairs.
  Flawed shortcut scales roots by opposing $a_i$: $X = x \times 10 \in \{40, 50\}$, $Y = y \times 1 \in \{6, 7\} \implies X > Y$ (false inversion!).

  ```javascript
  const xRoots = [4, 5];
  const yRoots = [6, 7];
  assert.strictEqual(xRoots.every(x => yRoots.every(y => x < y)), true);
  const scaledX = xRoots.map(x => x * 10);
  const scaledY = yRoots.map(y => y * 1);
  assert.strictEqual(scaledX.every(x => scaledY.every(y => x > y)), true);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 04: Quadratic Negative Constant CND Invariant
* **Target Domain**: Quadratic Equations & Sign Analysis (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-04
* **Proof**:
  If normalized equations have $c_1 < 0$ and $c_2 < 0$, their discriminants $D = b^2 - 4ac = b^2 + 4a|c| > 0$ are strictly positive, guaranteeing two distinct real roots for each equation.
  Because $x_1 x_2 = c_1/a_1 < 0$ and $y_1 y_2 = c_2/a_2 < 0$, both equations have one positive and one negative root:
  $$x_{\text{pos}} > 0 > x_{\text{neg}} \quad \text{and} \quad y_{\text{pos}} > 0 > y_{\text{neg}}$$
  Comparing cross pairs gives $x_{\text{pos}} > y_{\text{neg}}$ and $y_{\text{pos}} > x_{\text{neg}}$, guaranteeing mutual overlap (Cannot Be Determined).
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 02: Digital Root $\text{dr}(n)$ vs Residue Modulo 9 $r_9(n)$ Separation
* **Target Domain**: Mental Arithmetic & Divisibility Foundations (Ch 01)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-36
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Residue modulo 9 is $r_9(n) = n \bmod 9 \in \{0, 1, \dots, 8\}$.
  Digital Root is defined as $\text{dr}(0) = 0$, and for $n > 0$:
  $$\text{dr}(n) = 1 + ((n - 1) \bmod 9) \in \{1, 2, \dots, 9\}$$
  For any positive multiple of 9 ($n \in \{9, 18, 27, \dots\}$), $r_9(n) = 0$ while $\text{dr}(n) = 9$.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  function r9(n) { return n % 9; }
  function dr(n) { if (n === 0) return 0; return 1 + ((n - 1) % 9); }
  assert.strictEqual(r9(0), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(0), 0);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(9), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(9), 9);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(18), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(18), 9);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(14), 5); assert.strictEqual(dr(14), 5);
  [9, 18, 27, 36, 45, 90, 495].forEach(n => {
    assert.notStrictEqual(dr(n), r9(n));
    assert.strictEqual(dr(n), 9);
    assert.strictEqual(r9(n), 0);
  });
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 05: Quadratic Root Comparison Counterexample ($a_1 \neq a_2$)
* **Target Domain**: Quadratic Equations & Root Comparison (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-16
* **Counterexample to Root Cross-Scaling**:
  Consider $x^2 - 9x + 20 = 0 \implies x \in \{4, 5\}$ ($a_1 = 1$).
  And $10y^2 - 130y + 420 = 0 \implies y \in \{6, 7\}$ ($a_2 = 10$).
  True relation is $x < y$ for all root pairs.
  Flawed shortcut scales roots by opposing $a_i$: $X = x \times 10 \in \{40, 50\}$, $Y = y \times 1 \in \{6, 7\} \implies X > Y$ (false inversion!).

  const yRoots = [6, 7];
  assert.strictEqual(xRoots.every(x => yRoots.every(y => x < y)), true);
  const scaledX = xRoots.map(x => x * 10);
  const scaledY = yRoots.map(y => y * 1);
  assert.strictEqual(scaledX.every(x => scaledY.every(y => x > y)), true);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 06: Quadratic Negative Constant CND Invariant ($c_1 < 0, c_2 < 0$)
* **Target Domain**: Quadratic Equations & Root Comparison (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-04
* **Algebraic Proof of Straddling**:
  For $a > 0, c < 0$: product of roots is $P = c/a < 0$, guaranteeing one strictly positive root and one strictly negative root.
  Let $x_1 > 0 > x_2$ and $y_1 > 0 > y_2$.
  Then $x_1 > y_2$ (positive $>$ negative), while $y_1 > x_2$ (positive $>$ negative).
  This straddling guarantees overlapping intervals on the real number line, making $x > y$ and $x < y$ simultaneously impossible. Hence, the relation is strictly **Cannot Be Determined (CND)**.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  const a1 = 2, b1 = 3, c1 = -5;
  const a2 = 3, b2 = -4, c2 = -7;
  const x1 = 1, x2 = -2.5;
  const y1 = 2.35, y2 = -1.0;
  assert(x1 > 0 && x2 < 0 && y1 > 0 && y2 < 0);
  assert(x1 > y2 && y1 > x2);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 07: Rhombus Pythagorean Integrity (32, 126, 65)
* **Target Domain**: 2D Mensuration & Plane Geometry (Ch 22)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-17, ERR-39
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  In a rhombus, diagonals bisect each other at right angles:
  $$\text{Side } a = \sqrt{\left(\frac{d_1}{2}\right)^2 + \left(\frac{d_2}{2}\right)^2}$$
  Given Area $= \frac{1}{2} d_1 d_2 = 2016$ and $d_1 = 32 \implies d_2 = \frac{4032}{32} = 126$.
  Semi-diagonals are $16$ and $63$:
  $$a = \sqrt{16^2 + 63^2} = \sqrt{256 + 3969} = \sqrt{4225} = 65\text{ cm}$$
  Perimeter $= 4a = 260\text{ cm}$.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  const d1 = 32, area = 2016;
  const d2 = (2 * area) / d1; // 126
  assert.strictEqual(d2, 126);
  const side = Math.sqrt(Math.pow(d1/2, 2) + Math.pow(d2/2, 2)); // 65
  assert.strictEqual(side, 65);
  assert.strictEqual(4 * side, 260);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 02: Digital Root $\text{dr}(n)$ vs Residue Modulo 9 $r_9(n)$ Separation
* **Target Domain**: Mental Arithmetic & Divisibility Foundations (Ch 01)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-36
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Residue modulo 9 is $r_9(n) = n \bmod 9 \in \{0, 1, \dots, 8\}$.
  Digital Root is defined as $\text{dr}(0) = 0$, and for $n > 0$:
  $$\text{dr}(n) = 1 + ((n - 1) \bmod 9) \in \{1, 2, \dots, 9\}$$
  For any positive multiple of 9 ($n \in \{9, 18, 27, \dots\}$), $r_9(n) = 0$ while $\text{dr}(n) = 9$.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  function r9(n) { return n % 9; }
  function dr(n) { if (n === 0) return 0; return 1 + ((n - 1) % 9); }
  assert.strictEqual(r9(0), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(0), 0);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(9), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(9), 9);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(18), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(18), 9);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(14), 5); assert.strictEqual(dr(14), 5);
  [9, 18, 27, 36, 45, 90, 495].forEach(n => {
    assert.notStrictEqual(dr(n), r9(n));
    assert.strictEqual(dr(n), 9);
    assert.strictEqual(r9(n), 0);
  });
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 04: Modulo-100 Power Cyclicity ($76 \times 2^k$)
* **Target Domain**: Modular Arithmetic & Last Two Digits (Ch 07)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-02
* **Counterexample**:
  For $k = 1$: $76 \times 2^1 = 152 \equiv 52 \pmod{100}$, whereas $2^1 = 02 \pmod{100}$ ($52 \neq 02$).
* **Domain Boundary**:
  Valid strictly for integer $k \ge 2$:
  $$(76 - 1) \times 2^k = 75 \times 2^k = 75 \times 4 \times 2^{k-2} = 300 \times 2^{k-2} \equiv 0 \pmod{100}$$
  $$\implies 76 \times 2^k \equiv 2^k \pmod{100} \quad \forall k \ge 2$$
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual((76 * 2) % 100, 52);
  assert.notStrictEqual((76 * 2) % 100, 2);
  for (let k = 2; k <= 10; k++) {
    const val = (BigInt(76) * (BigInt(2) ** BigInt(k))) % 100n;
    const direct = (BigInt(2) ** BigInt(k)) % 100n;
    assert.strictEqual(val, direct);
  }
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

  True mathematical relationship: $x < y$ ($5 < 6$).
  Flawed cross-scaling shortcut ($X = x \cdot a_2, Y = y \cdot a_1$) produces $X \in \{40, 50\}$ and $Y \in \{6, 7\} \implies X > Y$, completely inverting the true result.

### TEST 06: Quadratic Negative Constant CND Invariant
* **Target Domain**: Algebra & Quadratic Root Signs (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-04
* **Mathematical Proof**:
  If $a_1, a_2 > 0$ and $c_1, c_2 < 0$, by Vieta's formulas the product of roots for each equation is $c_i / a_i < 0$. Therefore, each equation must possess exactly one strictly positive root and one strictly negative root:
  $$x_1 > 0 > x_2 \quad \text{and} \quad y_1 > 0 > y_2$$
  Comparing across equations: $x_1 > y_2$ (positive exceeds negative) while $y_1 > x_2$ (positive exceeds negative). Since neither set can dominate the other across all pairs, the relationship is universally **Cannot Be Determined (CND)**.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert(x1 > 0 && x2 < 0);
  assert(y1 > 0 && y2 < 0);
  assert(x1 > y2);
  assert(y1 > x2);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

* **Target Domain**: 2D Mensuration & Geometry (Ch 22)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-17, ERR-39
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Given area $2016\text{ cm}^2$ and diagonal $d_1 = 32\text{ cm}$:
  $$d_2 = \frac{2 \times \text{Area}}{d_1} = \frac{4032}{32} = 126\text{ cm}$$
  Diagonals bisect at right angles:
  $$\text{Side } a = \sqrt{\left(\frac{32}{2}\right)^2 + \left(\frac{126}{2}\right)^2} = \sqrt{16^2 + 63^2} = \sqrt{256 + 3969} = \sqrt{4225} = 65\text{ cm}$$
  $$\text{Perimeter } P = 4 \times 65 = 260\text{ cm}$$
* **Verification Assertion**:

### TEST 11: CI-SI Difference Ratio ($D_3 / D_2 = 3 + R/100$)
* **Target Domain**: Simple & Compound Interest (Ch 16)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-07
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  $$D_2 = P\left(\frac{R}{100}\right)^2, \quad D_3 = P\left(\frac{R}{100}\right)^2 \left(3 + \frac{R}{100}\right) \implies \frac{D_3}{D_2} = 3 + \frac{R}{100}$$
* **Verification Assertion**:

  const D3 = 310;
  assert.strictEqual(+(D3 / D2).toFixed(2), 3 + 10 / 100);
  ```

### TEST 13: General Complement Probability Identity & Dependent Counterexample
* **Target Domain**: Modern Mathematics & Probability (Ch 25)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-43
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Universal set-theoretic identity:
  $$P(A \cup B \cup C) = 1 - P(A' \cap B' \cap C')$$
  The product simplification $1 - P(A')P(B')P(C')$ is valid **if and only if** the events are statistically independent.
  Counterexample for dependent events:
  Let $A = B$ with $P(A) = P(B) = 0.5$. Then $P(A \cup B) = 0.5$.
  Applying the independent product shortcut yields $1 - (1 - 0.5)(1 - 0.5) = 1 - 0.25 = 0.75 \neq 0.5$.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  const pA = 0.5, pB = 0.5, pUnionActual = 0.5;
  const generalResult = 1 - 0.5; // P(A' intersect B') = P(A') = 0.5
  assert.strictEqual(generalResult, pUnionActual);
  const flawedProduct = 1 - (1 - pA) * (1 - pB);
  assert.notStrictEqual(flawedProduct, pUnionActual);
  assert.strictEqual(flawedProduct, 0.75);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

---e2:T4a3e,### TEST 01: Operator Precedence & "Of" Grouping
* **Target Domain**: Simplification & Arithmetic Expressions (Ch 02)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-01, ERR-34
* **Correct Mathematical Invariant**:
  Multiplication and Division share equal precedence, evaluated strictly left to right:
  $$20 \div 5 \times 2 = (20 \div 5) \times 2 = 4 \times 2 = 8$$
  "Of" represents an Indian competitive exam grouping convention with highest precedence:
  $$20 \div 5 \text{ of } 2 = 20 \div (5 \times 2) = 20 \div 10 = 2$$
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual((20 / 5) * 2, 8);
  assert.strictEqual(20 / (5 * 2), 2);
  assert.strictEqual(((180 / 15) / 3) * 4, 16);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 02: Digital Root $\text{dr}(n)$ vs Residue Modulo 9 $r_9(n)$ Separation
* **Target Domain**: Mental Arithmetic & Divisibility Foundations (Ch 01)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-36
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Residue modulo 9 is $r_9(n) = n \bmod 9 \in \{0, 1, \dots, 8\}$.
  Digital Root is defined as $\text{dr}(0) = 0$, and for $n > 0$:
  $$\text{dr}(n) = 1 + ((n - 1) \bmod 9) \in \{1, 2, \dots, 9\}$$
  For any positive multiple of 9 ($n \in \{9, 18, 27, \dots\}$), $r_9(n) = 0$ while $\text{dr}(n) = 9$.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  function r9(n) { return n % 9; }
  function dr(n) { if (n === 0) return 0; return 1 + ((n - 1) % 9); }
  assert.strictEqual(r9(0), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(0), 0);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(9), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(9), 9);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(18), 0); assert.strictEqual(dr(18), 9);
  assert.strictEqual(r9(14), 5); assert.strictEqual(dr(14), 5);
  [9, 18, 27, 36, 45, 90, 495].forEach(n => {
    assert.notStrictEqual(dr(n), r9(n));
    assert.strictEqual(dr(n), 9);
    assert.strictEqual(r9(n), 0);
  });
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 04: Modulo-100 Power Cyclicity ($76 \times 2^k$)
* **Target Domain**: Modular Arithmetic & Last Two Digits (Ch 07)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-02
* **Counterexample**:
  For $k = 1$: $76 \times 2^1 = 152 \equiv 52 \pmod{100}$, whereas $2^1 = 02 \pmod{100}$ ($52 \neq 02$).
* **Domain Boundary**:
  Valid strictly for integer $k \ge 2$:
  $$(76 - 1) \times 2^k = 75 \times 2^k = 75 \times 4 \times 2^{k-2} = 300 \times 2^{k-2} \equiv 0 \pmod{100}$$
  $$\implies 76 \times 2^k \equiv 2^k \pmod{100} \quad \forall k \ge 2$$
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual((76 * 2) % 100, 52);
  assert.notStrictEqual((76 * 2) % 100, 2);
  for (let k = 2; k <= 10; k++) {
    const val = (BigInt(76) * (BigInt(2) ** BigInt(k))) % 100n;
    const direct = (BigInt(2) ** BigInt(k)) % 100n;
    assert.strictEqual(val, direct);
  }
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 05: Quadratic Root Comparison & Anti-Cross-Scaling Refutation
* **Target Domain**: Algebra & Quadratic Comparisons (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-16
* **Counterexample to Cross-Scaling**:
  Eq 1: $x^2 - 9x + 20 = 0 \implies x \in \{4, 5\}$, $a_1 = 1$.
  Eq 2: $10y^2 - 130y + 420 = 0 \implies y \in \{6, 7\}$, $a_2 = 10$.
  True mathematical relationship: $x < y$ ($5 < 6$).
  Flawed cross-scaling shortcut ($X = x \cdot a_2, Y = y \cdot a_1$) produces $X \in \{40, 50\}$ and $Y \in \{6, 7\} \implies X > Y$, completely inverting the true result.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  const xRoots = [4, 5];
  const yRoots = [6, 7];
  assert.strictEqual(xRoots.every(x => yRoots.every(y => x < y)), true);
  const scaledX = xRoots.map(x => x * 10);
  const scaledY = yRoots.map(y => y * 1);
  assert.strictEqual(scaledX.every(x => scaledY.every(y => x > y)), true);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 06: Quadratic Negative Constant CND Invariant
* **Target Domain**: Algebra & Quadratic Root Signs (Ch 10)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-04
* **Mathematical Proof**:
  If $a_1, a_2 > 0$ and $c_1, c_2 < 0$, by Vieta's formulas the product of roots for each equation is $c_i / a_i < 0$. Therefore, each equation must possess exactly one strictly positive root and one strictly negative root:
  $$x_1 > 0 > x_2 \quad \text{and} \quad y_1 > 0 > y_2$$
  Comparing across equations: $x_1 > y_2$ (positive exceeds negative) while $y_1 > x_2$ (positive exceeds negative). Since neither set can dominate the other across all pairs, the relationship is universally **Cannot Be Determined (CND)**.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert(x1 > 0 && x2 < 0);
  assert(y1 > 0 && y2 < 0);
  assert(x1 > y2);
  assert(y1 > x2);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

  $$\text{Perimeter } P = 4 \times 65 = 260\text{ cm}$$

### TEST 13: Universal Complement Probability Identity, Sufficiency of Mutual Independence & Tested Counterexamples
* **Target Domain**: Modern Mathematics & Probability (Ch 25)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-43, ERR-50
* **Mathematical Invariants**:
  1. Universal set-theoretic identity:
     $$P(A \cup B \cup C) = 1 - P(A' \cap B' \cap C')$$
  2. Mutual independence is a **sufficient condition** for factoring the complement intersection into a product:
     $$P(A' \cap B' \cap C') = P(A')P(B')P(C')$$
  3. This equality does **NOT prove or imply independence** (non-equivalence).
  4. Counterexample 1 (Dependent events violating product shortcut):
     Let $A=B$ with $P(A)=P(B)=0.5$. $P(A \cup B) = 0.5$. Flawed shortcut gives $1 - (0.5)^2 = 0.75 \neq 0.5$.
  5. Counterexample 2 (Dependent events satisfying complement-product equality):
     On uniform 8-outcome sample space $S=\{1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8\}$, define:
     - $A = \{1,2,3,4\} \implies P(A') = 1/2$
     - $B = \{1,2,5,6\} \implies P(B') = 1/2$
     - $C = \{1,3,5,7\} \implies P(C') = 1/2$
     - Complements: $A' = \{5,6,7,8\}, B' = \{3,4,7,8\}, C' = \{2,4,6,8\}$
     - Triple intersection: $A' \cap B' \cap C' = \{8\} \implies P(A' \cap B' \cap C') = 1/8$
     - Product of complements: $P(A')P(B')P(C') = (1/2)^3 = 1/8$
     - Proves equality holds ($1/8 = 1/8$), yet equality alone does not prove independence.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  // Counterexample 1
  const pA = 0.5, pB = 0.5, pUnionActual = 0.5;
  const generalResult = 1 - 0.5;
  assert.strictEqual(generalResult, pUnionActual);
  const flawedProduct = 1 - (1 - pA) * (1 - pB);
  assert.strictEqual(flawedProduct, 0.75);
  assert.notStrictEqual(flawedProduct, pUnionActual);

  // Counterexample 2
  const pCompIntersection = 1 / 8;
  const pCompProduct = (1 / 2) * (1 / 2) * (1 / 2);
  assert.strictEqual(pCompIntersection, pCompProduct);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST 15: Number Series Practice Q8 (Cube Difference Sequence)
* **Target Domain**: Banking Number Series (Ch 04)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-20
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Series: $10, 18, 45, 110, 234, 450$.
  Differences: $8, 27, 65, 124, 216$. Cubes are $2^3=8, 3^3=27, 4^3=64, 5^3=125, 6^3=216$.
  Wrong term is **110** (should be $45 + 64 = 109$).
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  const correct = [10, 18, 45, 109, 234, 450];
  const given = [10, 18, 45, 110, 234, 450];
  assert.strictEqual(given[3], 110);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST 16: Number Series Practice Q10 (Second Differences)
* **Target Domain**: Banking Number Series (Ch 04)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-20, ERR-28
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Sequence: $100, 97, 90, 77, 56, 24$.
  Level 1 differences: $-3, -7, -13, -21, -32$.
  Level 2 differences: $-4, -6, -8, -11$.
  Pattern requires $-10 \implies$ next first difference is $-21 + (-10) = -31 \implies 56 - 31 = 25$.
  Corrupted term is **24** (should be 25).
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  const given = [100, 97, 90, 77, 56, 24];
  const d1 = [-3, -7, -13, -21, -32];
  const d2 = [-4, -6, -8, -11];
  assert.strictEqual(d1[3] + (-10), -31);
  assert.strictEqual(given[4] + (-31), 25);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST 17: Number Series Multi-Difference Hierarchy (14, 29, 52, 85, 130)
* **Target Domain**: Banking Number Series (Ch 04)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-21
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Level 1: $15, 23, 33, 45$. Level 2: $8, 10, 12$. Level 3: $2, 2$ (constant).

  const terms = [14, 29, 52, 85, 130];
  assert.deepStrictEqual(d3, [2, 2]);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

### TEST 18: Data Sufficiency Master Determination Rule
* **Target Domain**: Data Sufficiency Decision Logic (Ch 28)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-09, ERR-24
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  - Value question requires a single unique numerical value.
  - Yes/No question requires a definite YES or definite NO. Ambiguous outputs are insufficient.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual([-5, 5].length > 1, true); // ambiguous value -> insufficient
  assert.strictEqual([5].length === 1, true);   // unique value -> sufficient
  assert.strictEqual(['YES', 'YES'].every(o => o === 'YES'), true); // definite YES -> sufficient
  assert.strictEqual(['NO', 'NO'].every(o => o === 'NO'), true);   // definite NO -> sufficient
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST 19: Exponent Domain Scope & Counterexample Verification
* **Target Domain**: Powers, Indices & Surds (Ch 03)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-29
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Index laws assume positive real bases ($a > 0$).
  For negative bases, rational exponents fail power-of-power rules:
  $$\left((-1)^2\right)^{1/2} = 1^{1/2} = 1 \neq (-1)^{2 \times (1/2)} = (-1)^1 = -1$$
  Odd roots of negative reals remain real: $(-8)^{1/3} = -2$.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual(Math.pow(Math.pow(-1, 2), 0.5), 1);
  assert.strictEqual(Math.pow(-1, 2 * 0.5), -1);
  assert.strictEqual(-Math.cbrt(8), -2);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

### TEST 20: 3-Variable Overlapping Venn Caselet (1,200 Cohort)
* **Target Domain**: Advanced Mains Data Interpretation (Ch 27)
* **Defect Reference**: ERR-30
* **Mathematical Invariant**:
  Cohort of $1,200$ students. $R=650, Q=580, E=500$. Doubles: $RQ=260, QE=210, RE=220$. Triples: $100$.
  Disjoint regions:
  - Exact 2 only: $d=160, e=120, f=110$.
  - Exact 1 only: $a=270, b=210, c=170$.
  - Triples: $g=100$.
  - Passed at least one: $\sum = 1,140$.
  - Passed none: $1,200 - 1,140 = 60$. Total $= 1,140 + 60 = 1,200$.
* **Verification Assertion**:
  ```javascript
  assert.strictEqual(270 + 210 + 170 + 160 + 120 + 110 + 100, 1140);
  assert.strictEqual(1200 - 1140, 60);
  ```
* **Status**: **PASSED**

---

| **PAT-15** | Boats | Upstream/Downstream Round Trip Average Speed | $v_{\text{avg}} = \frac{u^2 - v^2}{u}$ (Harmonic mean of $u+v$ and $u-v$) | `[PYQ-DERIVED]` | SBI PO Prelims 2020 Archetype | Demonstrates why average round-trip speed in water is strictly less than still-water speed. |
| **PAT-16** | Mensuration | Solid Melting & Recasting Conservation | Recasting $N$ small spheres into one large cylinder or vice versa | `[MEMORY-BASED PYQ]` | IBPS PO Prelims 2022 | Volume conservation: $V_{\text{final}} = \sum V_{\text{initial}}$ with TSA increase. |
| **PAT-17** | Modern Math | Vowels Together / Never Together | Total permutations minus adjacent permutations | `[VERIFIED PYQ]` | SBI PO Prelims 2021 (Shift 3) | Tie method vs subtraction from total arrangements. |
| **PAT-18** | Probability | Defective Items with Replacement vs Without | Hypergeometric selection without replacement: $\frac{\binom{D}{k}\binom{N-D}{n-k}}{\binom{N}{n}}$ | `[MEMORY-BASED PYQ]` | RBI Grade B Phase I 2023 | Prevents binomial assumption when sampling without replacement. |
| **PAT-19** | DI (Table) | Missing Data Arithmetic Table DI | Table with missing production/sales data deducible via ratio constraints | `[MEMORY-BASED PYQ]` | SBI PO Mains 2022 | System of linear equations embedded in tabular layout. |
| **PAT-20** | DI (Caselet) | 3-Variable Overlapping Venn Diagram Caselet | Total $= n(A) + n(B) + n(C) - \sum \text{doubles} + \text{all three}$ | `[MEMORY-BASED PYQ]` | IBPS PO Mains 2021 | Deduces "only A and B", "exactly two", and "at least one" regions. |
| **PAT-21** | DI (Arithmetic) | Time & Work or Boat Stream Driven DI | Chart providing pipe flow rates or boat speeds across 5 rivers | `[EXAM-STYLE]` | Calibrated to SBI/IBPS PO Mains 2023 | High-yield Mains trend integrating arithmetic formulas into graphical sets. |
| **PAT-22** | Data Sufficiency | Value vs Yes/No Question Decision Tree | Verifying if constraints yield degrees of freedom $= 0$ without full computation | `[MEMORY-BASED PYQ]` | SBI PO Mains 2022 | Teaches definitive NO as sufficient, vs ambiguous/variable as insufficient. |
| **PAT-23** | Quantity Comp | 3-Quantity Comparison ($Q_1 \le Q_2 < Q_3$) | Evaluating roots, arithmetic outcomes, or probabilities across three independent quantities | `[MEMORY-BASED PYQ]` | IBPS PO Mains 2022 (Shift 1) | Simultaneous transitive comparison across three quantities under tight time. |
| **PAT-24** | Quadratic | Root Sign Invariant: Value on Real Line vs Absolute Magnitude | $x^2 + bx - c = 0 \implies x_{\text{pos}} > 0 > -x_{\text{neg}}$, so $x_{\text{pos}}$ is algebraically greater, $|-x_{\text{neg}}| > x_{\text{pos}}$ | `[EXAM-STYLE]` | Standardized Banking Invariant | Resolves sign-table confusion; enforces normalization $a > 0$ and $D \ge 0$. |
| **PAT-25** | Number Series | Cube-Difference Wrong Number Series | Sequence with differences $2^3, 3^3, 4^3, 5^3, 6^3$ where $T_k$ disrupts 2 differences | `[EXAM-STYLE]` | Calibrated to SBI PO Prelims 2023 | Demonstrates error-pivot isolation on non-linear polynomial differences. |
| **PAT-26** | DI Laboratory | Mains 3-Variable Venn Caselet (1,200 Candidates) | Caselet with multi-attribute constraints across Banking, SSC, and UPSC preparations | `[EXAM-STYLE]` | Calibrated to IBPS PO Mains 2023 | 5-question complete exam set with multi-step deductions and answer key. |

---eb:Teec,### 2.1 Commercial Arithmetic
1. **The Golden Pricing Bridge**:
   $$\frac{\text{MP}}{\text{CP}} = \frac{100 + P\%}{100 - D\%}$$
   *Condition*: Applicable whenever profit percentage $P\%$ and discount percentage $D\%$ are specified on the same transaction.
2. **Two Articles with Same SP and Equal $\pm x\%$ Gain/Loss**:
   $$\text{Net Loss}\% = \left(\frac{x}{10}\right)^2\% = \frac{x^2}{100}\%, \quad \text{Loss in ₹} = \frac{2 \cdot \text{SP} \cdot x^2}{10000 - x^2}$$
   *Condition*: Strictly requires **Selling Prices (SP) to be equal**. If Cost Prices are equal, net result is strictly $0\%$ (Breakeven)!
3. **Dishonest Merchant Universal Multiplier**:
   $$\text{Overall Profit Multiplier} = \left(\frac{100 + m}{100}\right) \times \left(\frac{100 - d}{100}\right) \times \left(\frac{\text{Claimed Weight}}{\text{Actual Weight}}\right)$$
   *Condition*: Valid for any combination of markup $m\%$, discount $d\%$, and faulty weight.
4. **Simple Interest Debt Installment**:
   $$n \cdot x + \frac{x \cdot R \cdot n(n-1)}{200} = A \implies x = \frac{200 \cdot A}{200n + R \cdot n(n-1)}$$
   *Condition*: $A$ is the **debt due at the end of $n$ years**. Not applicable to present borrowed loans (which require discounting).
5. **CI – SI Difference Invariants**:
   $$D_2 = P\left(\frac{R}{100}\right)^2, \quad \frac{D_3}{D_2} = 3 + \frac{R}{100}$$
   *Condition*: Constant annual rate $R$ compounded annually for 2 and 3 years respectively.
6. **Repeated Dilution / Replacement Invariant**:
   $$Q_{\text{final}} = Q_{\text{initial}}\left(1 - \frac{x}{V}\right)^n$$
   *Condition*: Total volume $V$ remains constant throughout all $n$ replacement cycles.

---

### 2.2 Rates, Work & Motion
7. **Phantom Worker Rule (Leaving Before Completion)**:
   $$T = \frac{W_{\text{actual}} + \eta_{\text{leaver}} \cdot d}{\sum \eta_{\text{all workers}}}$$
   *Condition*: When a worker leaves $d$ days *before* total project completion. Add their hypothetical work to total work!
8. **Alternating Work / Cistern Monkey Cycle Threshold**:
   $$q = \left\lceil \frac{V - a}{a - b} \right\rceil, \quad \text{Total Time} = 2q + \frac{V - q(a - b)}{a}$$
   *Condition*: Fill rate $a$, empty rate $b$. Must evaluate cycle threshold before the final forward leap!
9. **Cross-Meeting Kinematic Law**:
   $$\frac{v_A}{v_B} = \sqrt{\frac{T_B}{T_A}}, \quad t_{\text{meeting}} = \sqrt{T_A \cdot T_B}$$
   *Condition*: Two bodies start simultaneously, meet along the path, and take $T_A, T_B$ to reach destinations.
10. **Stream Vectors & Round-Trip Average Speed**:
    $$u = \frac{v_d + v_u}{2}, \quad v = \frac{v_d - v_u}{2}, \quad v_{\text{avg}} = \frac{u^2 - v^2}{u} = \frac{v_d \cdot v_u}{u}$$
    *Condition*: Round trip over identical distance upstream and downstream in steady current.

---

### 2.3 Algebra & Quadratics
11. **Quadratic Root Sign & Comparison Heuristic**:
    * $+bx + c = 0 \implies \text{Roots } (-,-)$
    * $-bx + c = 0 \implies \text{Roots } (+,+)$
    * $+bx - c = 0 \implies \text{Roots } (-,+)$ with negative root larger in magnitude
    * $-bx - c = 0 \implies \text{Roots } (+,-)$ with positive root larger in magnitude
    * **The Golden CND Rule**: If constant terms in **BOTH** equations are negative ($c_1 < 0$ and $c_2 < 0$), the relationship is **ALWAYS Cannot Be Determined (CND)**!

---

### 2.4 Mensuration & Modern Math
12. **Semicircle Perimeter & Solid Hemisphere TSA**:
    $$P_{\text{semicircle}} = \pi r + 2r = \frac{36}{7}r, \quad \text{TSA}_{\text{solid hemisphere}} = 3\pi r^2$$
13. **Inradius & Circumradius of Right-Angled Triangle**:
    $$r = \frac{a + b - c}{2}, \quad R = \frac{c}{2}$$
14. **Probability of "At Least One"**:
    $$P(\text{At least one}) = 1 - P(\text{None})$$
15. **Conditional Probability & Bayes' Rule**:
    $$P(A \mid B) = \frac{P(A \cap B)}{P(B)}$$

---ec:Tc47,1. **BODMAS "Of" Trap**: Evaluating $20 \div 5 \text{ of } 2$ as $(20 \div 5) \times 2 = 8$. "Of" takes grouping precedence: $20 \div (5 \times 2) = 2$. But for $20 \div 5 \times 2$, evaluate left-to-right $= 8$.
2. **Modulo 100 $k=1$ Trap**: Assuming $76 \times 2^k$ ends in $2^k$ for $k=1$. $76 \times 2 = 152 \equiv 52 \neq 02$. Condition is strictly $k \ge 2$.
3. **Same SP vs Same CP Trap**: Applying $(x/10)^2\%$ loss when items have the *same CP*. If CP is equal, $+x\%$ and $-x\%$ net exactly $0\%$.
4. **Dishonest Merchant Cheated Grams Base**: Calculating gain as $\frac{200}{1000} = 20\%$ when giving $800\text{g}$ for $1\text{kg}$. Correct base is actual weight delivered: $\frac{200}{800} = 25\%$.
5. **SI Debt Installment vs Borrowed Loan**: Treating $A$ as principal borrowed today instead of future debt due at year $n$.
6. **Compound Interest Nominal vs Half-Yearly**: For half-yearly compounding, rate is $R/2$ and time periods are $2n$. Do not double the rate!
7. **Pipes and Cisterns Final Leap Overshoot**: Multiplying net cycle progress blindly when the tank fills on an inlet hour before the drain opens.
8. **Relative Speed Collinear vs Opposite**: Subtracting speeds when trains travel towards each other. Opposite directions $\implies v_1 + v_2$; Same direction $\implies |v_1 - v_2|$.
9. **Average Speed Arithmetic Mean Fallacy**: Calculating average speed as $\frac{v_1 + v_2}{2}$ for equal distances. Must use harmonic mean: $\frac{2v_1 v_2}{v_1 + v_2}$.
10. **Trains Crossing Length Omission**: Forgetting that crossing a platform or bridge covers distance $L_{\text{train}} + L_{\text{platform}}$, whereas crossing a pole covers $L_{\text{train}}$ only.
11. **Boats Still Water Speed Confusion**: Assuming upstream speed is $u$ instead of $u - v$.
12. **Semicircle Perimeter Diameter Omission**: Writing $\pi r$ instead of $\pi r + 2r = \frac{36}{7}r$.
13. **Solid Hemisphere Flat Face Omission**: Writing curved surface area $2\pi r^2$ instead of Total Surface Area $3\pi r^2$.
14. **Quadratic Leading Coefficient Scaling Omission**: Comparing raw roots $(x_1, x_2)$ and $(y_1, y_2)$ when leading coefficients $a_1 \neq a_2$. Must scale by opposing $a_i$ or divide.
15. **Data Sufficiency "Sufficiency" vs "Truth"**: Rejecting a statement because it proves the answer is definitively "NO". In DS, a definitive "NO" is 100% SUFFICIENT!
16. **Data Sufficiency Combining Without Checking Alone**: Combining Statement 1 and 2 before verifying whether Statement 1 or 2 is individually sufficient.
17. **Number Series Two-Level Difference Overlook**: Stopping at the first difference when a sequence appears erratic. Check second and third differences!
18. **Approximation Aggressive Rounding with Close Options**: Rounding $14.8$ to $15$ and $24.2$ to $24$ when options differ by only $1$ unit. Check option spacing first!
19. **Dual Pie Chart Base Mismatch**: Subtracting or adding percentages directly from two pie charts that represent different absolute totals.
20. **At Least One Probability Complement Omission**: Summing multiple complicated probability cases instead of calculating $1 - P(\text{None})$.

  $1^3=1$, $2^3=8$, $3^3=27$, $4^3=64$, $5^3=125$, $6^3=216$, $7^3=343$, $8^3=512$, $9^3=729$, $10^3=1000$,

---ef:T1317,### 2.1 Commercial Arithmetic
1. **The Golden Pricing Bridge**:
   $$\frac{\text{MP}}{\text{CP}} = \frac{100 + P\%}{100 - D\%}$$
   *Condition*: Applicable whenever profit percentage $P\%$ and discount percentage $D\%$ are specified on the same transaction.
2. **Two Articles with Same SP and Equal $\pm x\%$ Gain/Loss**:
   $$\text{Net Loss}\% = \left(\frac{x}{10}\right)^2\% = \frac{x^2}{100}\%, \quad \text{Loss in ₹} = \frac{2 \cdot \text{SP} \cdot x^2}{10000 - x^2}$$
   *Condition*: Strictly requires **Selling Prices (SP) to be equal**. If Cost Prices are equal, net result is strictly $0\%$ (Breakeven)!
3. **Dishonest Merchant Universal Multiplier**:
   $$\text{Overall Profit Multiplier} = \left(\frac{100 + m}{100}\right) \times \left(\frac{100 - d}{100}\right) \times \left(\frac{\text{Claimed Weight}}{\text{Actual Weight}}\right)$$
   *Condition*: Valid for any combination of markup $m\%$, discount $d\%$, and faulty weight.
4. **Simple Interest Debt Installment**:
   $$n \cdot x + \frac{x \cdot R \cdot n(n-1)}{200} = A \implies x = \frac{200 \cdot A}{200n + R \cdot n(n-1)}$$
   *Condition*: $A$ is the **debt due at the end of $n$ years**. Not applicable to present borrowed loans (which require discounting).
5. **CI – SI Difference Invariants**:
   $$D_2 = P\left(\frac{R}{100}\right)^2, \quad \frac{D_3}{D_2} = 3 + \frac{R}{100}$$
   *Condition*: Constant annual rate $R$ compounded annually for 2 and 3 years respectively.
6. **Repeated Dilution / Replacement Invariant**:
   $$Q_{\text{final}} = Q_{\text{initial}}\left(1 - \frac{x}{V}\right)^n$$
   *Condition*: Total volume $V$ remains constant throughout all $n$ replacement cycles.

---

### 2.2 Work & Kinematics
1. **Leaving Days Before Completion (Phantom Worker)**:
   $$T = \frac{W_{\text{actual}} + \eta_{\text{leaver}} \cdot d}{\sum \eta}$$
   *Condition*: Worker leaves $d$ days before scheduled completion; phantom work added to total units.
2. **Alternating Work / Pipes Cycle Threshold**:
   $$q = \left\lceil \frac{V - a}{a - b} \right\rceil$$
   *Condition*: Stop cycling before water level reaches within single-step filling capacity $a$ to prevent negative cycle overshoot.
3. **Kinematic Meeting & Destination Speeds**:
   $$\frac{v_A}{v_B} = \sqrt{\frac{T_B}{T_A}}, \quad t_{\text{meeting}} = \sqrt{T_A T_B}$$
   *Condition*: Travelers start simultaneously, meet at intermediate point, and take $T_A, T_B$ to reach respective destinations.
4. **Round-Trip Stream Average Speed**:
   $$v_{\text{avg}} = \frac{u^2 - v^2}{u} = \frac{2(u+v)(u-v)}{(u+v)+(u-v)}$$
   *Condition*: Distance downstream equals distance upstream ($D_{\text{down}} = D_{\text{up}}$).

---

### 2.3 Geometry & Mensuration
1. **Semicircle Perimeter**:
   $$P = \pi r + 2r = \frac{36}{7}r \quad (\text{for } \pi \approx 22/7)$$
   *Condition*: Closed semicircle including the straight diameter baseline.
2. **Solid Hemisphere Total Surface Area**:
   $$\text{TSA} = 2\pi r^2 (\text{Curved}) + \pi r^2 (\text{Flat Top}) = 3\pi r^2$$
   *Condition*: Solid solid body, not hollow thin-shell hemispherical bowl.
3. **Cone Frustum Volume**:
   $$V = \frac{1}{3}\pi h (R^2 + r^2 + Rr), \quad l = \sqrt{h^2 + (R - r)^2}$$
4. **Rhombus Invariants**:
   $$\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2}d_1 d_2, \quad 4a^2 = d_1^2 + d_2^2 \implies a = \frac{1}{2}\sqrt{d_1^2 + d_2^2}$$

---

### 2.4 Algebra & Quadratics
1. **The Sign Table Invariants ($x^2 \pm bx \pm c = 0$)**:
   * $(+, +) \implies (-, -)$
   * $(-, +) \implies (+, +)$
   * $(+, -) \implies (- \text{ larger}, + \text{ smaller})$
   * $(-, -) \implies (+ \text{ larger}, - \text{ smaller})$
2. **The Instant CND Theorem**:
   $$\text{If } c_1 < 0 \text{ and } c_2 < 0 \implies \mathbf{CND} \text{ (Relationship Cannot Be Determined)}$$
   *Proof*: Each equation has 1 positive and 1 negative root. Inequalities cross ($x_1 > -y_2$ and $y_1 > -x_2$); no global order is possible.
3. **Root Comparison with Different Leading Coefficients ($a_1 \neq a_2$)**:
   * Method 1: Convert roots $\alpha = N_1/a_1, \beta = N_2/a_2$ to decimals directly.
   * Method 2: Scale un-divided factor numerators by opposing $a_i$. Never cross-scale already-divided roots!

---

### 2.5 Data Sufficiency & Modern Math
1. **The Definitive "NO" Sufficiency Rule**:
   $$\text{Definitive YES} \implies \text{Sufficient!} \quad \text{Definitive NO} \implies \mathbf{Sufficient!}$$
   *Only ambiguous / dual outcomes (Yes AND No) constitute Insufficiency.*
2. **Sufficiency Computation Rule**:
   *Do not compute more than necessary to establish sufficiency!* The moment non-redundant equations guarantee unique degrees of freedom $= 0$, STOP immediately.
3. **At Least One Complement**:
   $$P(\text{At least one success}) = 1 - P(\text{Zero successes})$$
4. **Bayes' Theorem Posterior Probability**:
   $$P(B_i \mid A) = \frac{P(B_i) \cdot P(A \mid B_i)}{\sum_{j=1}^k P(B_j) \cdot P(A \mid B_j)}$$

---f0:Tcf4,1. **BODMAS "Of" Confusion**: Evaluating $20 \div 5 \text{ of } 2 = 8$. Correct: $20 \div (5 \times 2) = 2$.
2. **Modulo 100 $k=1$ Boundary**: Assuming $76 \times 2^k$ ends in $2^k$ for $k=1$. True strictly for $k \ge 2$.
3. **Same SP vs Same CP**: Assuming $(x/10)^2\%$ loss when CP is equal. If CP is equal, net profit/loss is $0\%$.
4. **Dishonest Weight Base**: Using 1000g as denominator instead of actual 800g delivered.
5. **SI Installment Debt Base**: Mistaking future debt due for loan borrowed today.
6. **Compound Interest Half-Yearly Rate**: Doubling the rate instead of halving it ($R/2$).
7. **Pipes and Cisterns Monkey Cycle Overshoot**: Calculating net cycles beyond the final positive filling leap.
8. **Relative Speed Opposite Directions**: Subtracting speeds instead of adding.
9. **Average Speed Arithmetic Fallacy**: Averaging speeds $\frac{v_1 + v_2}{2}$ instead of harmonic mean for equal distances.
10. **Train Passing Platform Distance**: Forgetting to add platform length to train length.
11. **Semicircle Perimeter Diameter Omission**: Writing $\pi r$ instead of $\pi r + 2r = \frac{36}{7}r$.
12. **Solid Hemisphere Flat Top Omission**: Writing $2\pi r^2$ instead of Total Surface Area $3\pi r^2$.
13. **Quadratic Leading Coefficient Root Cross-Scaling**: Multiplying already-divided roots by opposing $a_i$ instead of decimal comparison.
14. **Quadratic Negative Constants Fallacy**: Forgetting that both $c_1 < 0$ and $c_2 < 0 \implies \text{CND}$ without solving!

17. **Approximation Aggressive Rounding with Close Options**: Rounding when option gap is $< 1\%$.

20. **Number Series Two-Level Difference Neglect**: Giving up after Level 1 difference without checking Level 2.

22. **Partnership Sleeping Partner Commission Omission**: Distributing total profit before deducting active manager share.
23. **Cube Recasting Surface Area Increase**: Assuming surface area is conserved during melting (only volume is conserved).
24. **Inradius Right Triangle Hypotenuse Confusion**: Using $a + b + c$ instead of $a + b - c$ over 2.
25. **Chained Division Right-to-Left Error**: Evaluating $a \div b \div c$ from right to left.
26. **Circular Track Co-prime Reduction Omission**: Finding meeting points without reducing speeds to lowest coprime terms.
27. **Missing Data Table Linear Inconsistency**: Filling blanks without cross-verifying row and column totals.
28. **Quantity Comparison Transitive Blindness**: Forgetting that if $Q_1 > Q_2$ and $Q_2 < Q_3$, relation between $Q_1$ and $Q_3$ cannot be determined.
29. **Sub-base 200 Multiplier Omission**: Forgetting to multiply LHS by 2 in Base 200 multiplication.

Population Compound Growth\nLet present population be $P$, growing at annual rate $R\\%$:\n* **Population after $n$ years**:\n  $$P_n = \\mathbf{P\\left(1 + \\frac{R}{100}\\right)^n}$$\n* **Population $n$ years ago**:\n  $$P_{\\text{past}} = \\mathbf{\\frac{P}{\\left(1 + \\frac{R}{100}\\right)^n}}$$\n\n---\n\n### 2. If its present population is 185,220, what was it 3 years ago?\n* Here $R = 5\\% = \\frac{1}{20} \\implies$ Multiplier is $\\left(1 + \\frac{1}{20}\\right) = \\frac{21}{20}$.\n* Population 3 years ago:\n  $$P_0 = 185220 \\div \\left(\\frac{21}{20}\\right)^3 = 185220 \\times \\frac{8000}{9261}$$\n* Since $185220 / 9261 = 20$:\n  $$P_0 = 20 \\times 8000 = \\mathbf{160,000}$$\n\n---","wordCount":140,"itemCount":"$undefined","itemLabel":"140 words"},{"id":"sec-6","slug":"sec-6","title":"11.6 Top Examiner Traps in Percentage Questions","order":6,"body":"1. **The Election \"Total Votes\" vs \"Valid Votes\" Ambiguity**:\n   * If question says *\"A candidate got 60% of the VALID votes\"*, calculate the base after subtracting invalid votes.\n   * If question says *\"A candidate got 60% of the TOTAL votes polled\"*, the base includes invalid votes!\n2. **The Successive Discount Addition Fallacy**:\n   Two successive discounts of $20\\%$ and $10\\%$ do NOT equal a $30\\%$ discount!  \n   $$\\text{Net Discount} = 20 + 10 - \\frac{20 \\times 10}{100} = 30 - 2 = \\mathbf{28\\%}$$\n3. **The Passing Mark Margin Confusion**:\n   If candidate $A$ gets $30\\%$ and fails by $10$ marks, while candidate $B$ gets $40\\%$ and gets $15$ marks more than the pass mark:\n   $$\\text{Difference in \\%} = 40\\% - 30\\% = 10\\%$$\n   $$\\text{Difference in Marks} = (+15) - (-10) = \\mathbf{25 \\text{ marks}} \\quad \\text{(NOT } 15 - 10 = 5!\\text{)}$$\n   $$10\\% = 25 \\implies \\text{Total Marks} = \\mathbf{250}$$.","wordCount":147,"itemCount":"$undefined","itemLabel":"147 words"}],"preamble":"**Canonical Sources Unified**:\n* Dr. R.S. The **Assumed Mean Method** chooses a convenient benchmark number $A$ proximate to the cluster, calculating deviations:\n\n$$\\mathbf{\\bar{X} = A + \\frac{\\sum_{i=1}^{n} (x_i - A)}{n}}$$\n\n#### Exemplar: Find the average of $894, 912, 885, 921, 908$.\n1. Compute individual deviations $(x_i - 900)$:\n   * $894 - 900 = -6$\n   * $912 - 900 = +12$\n   * $885 - 900 = -15$\n   * $921 - 900 = +21$\n   * $908 - 900 = +8$\n3. Sum of deviations:\n   $$\\sum d_i = -6 + 12 - 15 + 21 + 8 = +20$$\n4. Net adjustment: $\\frac{+20}{5} = +4$.\n5. True Average $= 900 + 4 = \\mathbf{904}$.\n\n---","wordCount":122,"itemCount":"$undefined","itemLabel":"122 words"},{"id":"sec-3","slug":"sec-3","title":"13.3 Weighted Mean & Multi-Group Aggregation","order":3,"body":"When combining two groups with counts $n_1, n_2$ and averages $\\bar{X}_1, \\bar{X}_2$:\n\n$$\\mathbf{\\bar{X}_{\\text{combined}} = \\frac{n_1\\bar{X}_1 + n_2\\bar{X}_2}{n_1 + n_2}}$$\n\n### The Inverse Weight Ratio Law:\nRearranging the combined mean equation establishes:\n$$n_1(\\bar{X}_{\\text{combined}} - \\bar{X}_1) = n_2(\\bar{X}_2 - \\bar{X}_{\\text{combined}})$$\n\n$$\\mathbf{\\frac{n_1}{n_2} = \\frac{\\bar{X}_2 - \\bar{X}_{\\text{combined}}}{\\bar{X}_{\\text{combined}} - \\bar{X}_1}}$$\n\n*(This is the exact mathematical foundation of the Alligation Cross-Rule!)*\n\n---","wordCount":56,"itemCount":"$undefined","itemLabel":"56 words"},{"id":"sec-4","slug":"sec-4","title":"13.4 Insertion, Departure & Replacement Dynamics","order":4,"body":"$51","wordCount":208,"itemCount":"$undefined","itemLabel":"208 words"},{"id":"sec-5","slug":"sec-5","title":"13.5 Consecutive Numbers & Arithmetic Progressions","order":5,"body":"$52","wordCount":188,"itemCount":"$undefined","itemLabel":"188 words"},{"id":"sec-6","slug":"sec-6","title":"13.6 Cricket Batting & Bowling Average Analytics","order":6,"body":"$53","wordCount":167,"itemCount":"$undefined","itemLabel":"167 words"},{"id":"sec-7","slug":"sec-7","title":"13.7 Top Examiner Traps in Averages","order":7,"body":"1. **The Bowling Average \"Improvement\" Sign Trap**:\n   When a question states *\"a bowler's average improves by 0.5 runs\"*, students add $0.5$.
